## 119 · ChatGPT · 2026-09-22 18:23:39 PDT

<!-- source-message-id: 6ee8f4c9-87cd-4a3b-9ca1-7d9091a78834 -->

Exactly. **One economic generation deep, unlimited attribution depth.**

If Jon forks Sarah's CR 701 into CR 936, Sarah receives the **Source Creator Cut** on CR 936 itself **and every Variation within the 936 family**. If Mike later forks CR 936 into CR 1204, Jon becomes the Source Creator for 1204. Sarah remains permanently visible in the ancestry, but her economic participation doesn't cascade into 1204.

I've updated the terminology throughout and removed the old Remixer/Sub-Remixer ambiguity.

:::writing{variant="document" id="58314" title="CultRemix Product Thesis & V1 Build Plan"}
# CultRemix

## Product Thesis

**Culture itself is a remix.**

Every artist, designer, musician, filmmaker, writer, brand, and movement builds on what came before it. We borrow ideas, reinterpret aesthetics, combine influences, change context, and make things our own. We stand on the shoulders of the people who created before us, and culture moves forward because each generation remixes what it inherits.

The saying often attributed to Pablo Picasso captures the spirit: **“Good artists copy, great artists steal.”** The point isn't literal copying. Great creative work absorbs influences so completely that they become the raw material for something new.

AI changes the scale at which this can happen.

Take a great typography shirt. Historically, a designer creates one phrase in one visual treatment and sells that finished design.

**But what if that finished design isn't the end of the creative process? What if it's just the beginning?**

That same composition, typography, texture, illustration, and visual language could work with thousands of different phrases. One person might love the design but want completely different words. **Another might change the phrase and accidentally create the version that goes viral.**

**One great creative idea can become the starting point for thousands of others.**

Two technologies have now matured at exactly the right time to make that possible at scale.

**Generative AI dramatically expands what we can create and how quickly we can create it. At the same time, modern print-on-demand has become a high-quality, globally scalable manufacturing and fulfillment layer, allowing each new creation to become a real physical product without producing or holding inventory in advance.**

Now, we no longer have to manufacture one static interpretation of an idea and hope everyone wants the same thing.

**We can embrace infinite remixing.**

A Creator can make a great design and define which parts other people can make their own. Someone can remix the words and create a new Variation. A sufficiently transformative interpretation can fork into an entirely new creative root with its own remixable system. **Every finished Variation immediately becomes a real product that can be purchased, shared, promoted, and sold.**

And CultRemix remembers where all of it came from.

> **CultRemix is a creative commerce platform where every work remembers where it came from and everyone who contributes to its value can participate in its economics.**

Creators publish remixable works. Other people create Variations or fork them into new creative roots. Brands can open portions of their identity to remixing. Affiliates distribute the resulting products. **CultRemix is built on the Monad blockchain, using permanent onchain provenance and programmable royalties to preserve creative lineage and ensure value flows back to the people who helped create it.**

## The Core Principle

> **Don’t just make something people like. Make something people want to make their own.**

That changes the goal of creation.

A successful CultRemix isn't merely something that gets likes. It's something that inspires participation.

**“I love that.” becomes “I want my version.”**

And every act of participation can create something new.

---

# Core Creative Model

CultRemix uses a small set of precise concepts.

## CultRemix

A **CultRemix** is a canonical remixable creative system or creative root.

It contains the creative work itself, its Remix schema, Creator, provenance, economics, products, and lineage.

## Variation

A **Variation** is a new expression created within an existing CultRemix's permitted Remix schema.

If a typography CultRemix allows the phrase and color to change, someone changing those variables creates a Variation.

The underlying creative system remains the same.

## Fork

A **Fork** intentionally uses an existing CultRemix as creative source material but establishes a sufficiently new creative system of its own.

A Fork receives a new CultRemix root and its own Remix schema.

It remains permanently connected to its direct parent through provenance.

## Creator

The person or entity that creates a CultRemix root.

## Variation Creator

The person who creates a particular Variation within an existing CultRemix.

## Source Creator

When one CultRemix is forked into another, the Creator of the **direct parent CultRemix** becomes the Source Creator of the new Fork.

The Source Creator can participate economically in that Fork and its Variations.

## Brand / Rights Holder

An optional entity whose authorized Brand Assets or creative world are being used.

## Affiliate / Promoter

The person attributed with causing a qualifying transaction.

## Remix

**Remix** is primarily the action.

The user presses:

> **↻ REMIX THIS**

and ordinarily creates a **Variation**.

If they substantially transform the creative system itself, they can instead create a **Fork**, which becomes a new CultRemix root.

---

# The Creative Graph

Every published work receives a permanent CultRemix identity.

For example:

**CULTREMIX № 701-001**  
**AKA “Death Before Decaf”**

The number is canonical.

The AKA is optional and human.

`701` identifies the creative family.

`001` identifies the Creator's canonical first expression of it.

Someone presses:

> **REMIX THIS ↻**

and changes the phrase.

The result might be:

**CULTREMIX № 701-002**  
**AKA “Sleep Is for Quitters”**

It remains inside the **701** creative family.

If someone substantially transforms CR 701 into a new remixable creative system, the result might become:

**CULTREMIX № 936-001**

with metadata:

> **Forked from CR № 701-042**

CR 936 now has its own Variations:

**936-002**  
**936-003**  
**936-004**

The creative graph might therefore look like:

**701-001**  
↓ Variation  
**701-002**  
↓ Variation  
**701-042**  
↓ Fork  
**936-001**  
↓ Variation  
**936-002**

And someone might eventually Fork 936:

**1204-001**  
*Forked from CR 936*

The complete ancestry remains visible forever:

**1204 → 936 → 701**

> **CultRemix remembers where creativity came from.**

---

# Infinite Attribution, Bounded Economics

This is a fundamental CultRemix principle:

> **Creative lineage can be infinitely deep. Economic inheritance is deliberately bounded.**

Suppose Sarah creates:

**CR 701**

Jon Forks it into:

**CR 936**

Sarah becomes the **Source Creator** of CR 936.

Sarah receives the applicable Source Creator Cut when:

- CR 936-001 sells
- CR 936-002 sells
- CR 936-300 sells
- any other qualifying Variation within the CR 936 family sells

Jon remains the Creator of CR 936.

If Mike later Forks CR 936 into:

**CR 1204**

Jon becomes the Source Creator of CR 1204.

Sarah remains permanently credited in the creative ancestry:

**1204 → 936 → 701**

but Sarah's Source Creator economics do **not** automatically cascade into CR 1204.

This creates a simple rule:

> **Source Creator economics travel exactly one Fork generation deep. Provenance travels forever.**

That prevents infinitely recursive royalty trees while ensuring someone cannot escape a Creator's economics simply by making enough changes to call something a Fork.

---

# Original, Variation, or Fork?

CultRemix should not pretend that AI can perfectly adjudicate originality.

Instead, publishing combines Creator declarations, structural rules, automated similarity analysis, and review when necessary.

## Variation

A work uses the existing CultRemix schema.

For example:

Existing schema permits:

**Phrase**  
**Color**

Someone changes those values.

That is clearly a Variation.

## Fork

Someone intentionally starts from an existing CultRemix but changes the creative system itself.

They may change:

- composition
- visual hierarchy
- typography system
- illustrations
- graphic language
- available Remix variables
- relationships among those variables

The resulting work establishes its own Remix schema.

It becomes a new CultRemix root while retaining its parent relationship.

## Independent CultRemix

A Creator can also publish something as an independent creative root.

CultRemix can compare it against existing works using:

- visual similarity
- structural similarity
- typography similarity
- composition
- conceptual similarity
- Remix-schema similarity
- known source material

If a meaningful relationship appears, CultRemix can say:

> **Possible creative lineage detected: CR № 701**

The Creator can:

**Create as a Variation**

**Create as a Fork**

or

**Assert Independent Creation**

High-confidence or disputed cases can be reviewed.

---

# Lineage Corrections

Attribution will sometimes be discovered after publication.

Suppose:

**CR № 936-001**

is initially published as independent.

Later CultRemix determines that it substantially derives from:

**CR № 701-042**

CultRemix can establish:

**701-042 → 936-001**

without erasing history.

Monad retains the original provenance event and records a subsequent lineage amendment.

The record therefore accurately reflects:

> CR 936 was originally registered as independent.

and later:

> CR 936 was determined to be a Fork of CR 701.

Future economic events can use the corrected Source Creator relationship.

Historical settlements are not silently rewritten.

Economic configurations are versioned so the platform can determine which rules applied to each transaction at the time it occurred.

**History is preserved. Attribution improves.**

---

# Programmable Creative Economics

CultRemix recognizes that different people can contribute different kinds of value to the same cultural object.

For product commerce, there are five potential economic roles.

## 1. Brand / Rights Holder

Optional.

A Brand receives economics when authorized Brand Assets or Brand Worlds participate in the work.

## 2. Source Creator

Optional.

Exists when the current CultRemix is a Fork.

The Source Creator is the Creator of the direct parent CultRemix.

The Source Creator participates economically in the Fork and qualifying Variations belonging to that Fork's creative family.

Source Creator economics extend exactly one Fork generation.

## 3. Creator

The Creator of the current CultRemix root.

## 4. Variation Creator

Optional.

The person who created the specific Variation being purchased.

## 5. Affiliate / Promoter

Optional.

The person attributed with causing the transaction.

So a transaction can support:

> **Brand → Source Creator → Creator → Variation Creator → Affiliate**

Each role represents a different contribution.

One person or entity can occupy multiple roles.

---

# Example: Brand-Created World

Suppose a brand creates an official CultRemix.

**CR 701-001**

Brand: **Brand X**

Creator: **Brand X**

Jon creates:

**CR 701-382**

Brand: **Brand X**

Creator: **Brand X**

Variation Creator: **Jon**

Theo promotes it and causes a sale.

Affiliate: **Theo**

Brand X occupies both Brand and Creator roles.

Jon receives Variation Creator economics.

Theo receives Affiliate economics.

---

# Example: Fork Economics

Jon creates a permitted Fork:

**CR 936-001**

derived from:

**CR 701**

The economics can now include:

Brand: **Brand X**

Source Creator: **Brand X / CR 701**

Creator: **Jon / CR 936**

Sarah then creates:

**CR 936-204**

Now:

Brand: **Brand X**

Source Creator: **Brand X / CR 701**

Creator: **Jon / CR 936**

Variation Creator: **Sarah**

Affiliate: **Theo**

All five economic roles are now populated for one transaction.

If CR 936 is later Forked into CR 1204, Jon becomes Source Creator for 1204.

The earlier Source Creator relationship does not economically cascade another generation.

> **Lineage can be infinite. Economics remain bounded.**

---

# Brand Worlds

Brands can eventually open CultRemix Worlds.

Rather than simply protecting every part of an identity, a Brand can say:

> **“Here’s the part of our identity we’re inviting culture to play with.”**

A Brand World can contain approved:

- logos
- word marks
- phrases
- characters
- visual systems
- likenesses
- photography
- graphic elements
- other Brand Assets

Each asset can have Remix permissions.

Possible modes include:

**Open**

Anyone can use the asset within defined rules.

**Approval Required**

Creators can create with it, but commercial publication requires Brand approval.

**Invited Creators**

Only selected Creators can publish commercial work.

**Closed**

The asset is protected from third-party commercial use within CultRemix.

Brands can also determine whether:

- Variations are allowed
- Forks are allowed
- Brand approval is required
- products can be sold
- certain categories are prohibited
- specific royalty configurations apply

A Brand can elevate exceptional community work to:

> **OFFICIAL ✓**

This turns Brand identity into programmable creative material.

---

# Creative Rights & Brand Assets

CultRemix distinguishes between three related systems.

## Creative Lineage

Who created this creative system and what did it descend from?

## Brand Assets

Whose authorized logo, phrase, visual identity, likeness, character, or other Brand material is incorporated?

## External Rights

Does outside intellectual property potentially exist that CultRemix needs to recognize?

CultRemix can use automated analysis and external search systems to detect:

- trademarked phrases
- logos
- recognizable Brand marks
- substantially similar existing CultRemixes
- potentially protected external material

High-confidence issues can be blocked or routed for review.

Ambiguous cases can use a rights-holder reporting and review process.

CultRemix should never imply that paying to register a phrase internally grants legal ownership of that phrase.

Platform Brand Asset rights and actual trademark rights remain distinct.

---

# Affiliate & Creator Referral Economics

Affiliate economics extend beyond product sales.

When Creators livestream, post creation videos, share their work, promote their profiles, or simply demonstrate what they're making, they are also marketing CultRemix.

Someone may watch and think:

> **“I want to do this too.”**

If that person becomes a paying CultRemix Creator, the person who caused that conversion created measurable ecosystem value.

CultRemix therefore supports two major affiliate earning categories.

## Commerce Referrals

An Affiliate causes a customer to purchase a product.

The Affiliate receives the applicable product commission.

## Creator Referrals

An Affiliate causes someone to become a paying Creator.

The Affiliate receives a **recurring share of eligible Creator subscription revenue** under the applicable referral program.

The exact percentage and duration remain configurable.

For example:

> **20% of referred Creator subscription revenue for 12 months.**

Referral attribution is direct rather than multilevel.

If Sarah refers Jane and Jane later refers Mike:

**Sarah earns from Jane.**

**Jane earns from Mike.**

Sarah does not receive an override from Mike.

The Creator proposition becomes:

> **Create. Get remixed. Get paid.**

Creators can earn when:

- their CultRemix sells
- Variations within their creative family sell
- their own Variations sell
- their direct Fork descendants generate qualifying sales
- they drive product sales
- they directly inspire paying Creators to join CultRemix

---

# Affiliate Links, Codes & Offers

Every eligible Creator or Affiliate can have simple attribution tools for social posts, livestreams, Stories, profiles, videos, and offline promotion.

Two primary mechanisms represent the same Affiliate identity.

## Tracking Link

Example:

`cultremix.com/r/701?ref=theo`

## Affiliate Code

Example:

**THEO**

The code simultaneously provides:

### Customer Incentive

> **Use THEO for 10% off.**

### Affiliate Attribution

The qualifying transaction is attributed to Theo.

Codes are especially useful when a link isn't practical.

Someone may:

- watch a livestream on television
- hear a Creator say the code
- change devices
- discover a repost
- remember a Creator's name
- visit CultRemix later directly

The code preserves attribution.

## Short Codes

Creators can provide a preferred base:

**Theo**

CultRemix generates the shortest appropriate available code:

**THEO**

If unavailable:

**THEO10**

or another concise approved alternative.

CultRemix controls final code issuance and reserves misleading, administrative, platform, and unauthorized Brand terms.

## Super Admin Controls Offers

Affiliates do not determine discounts.

Super Admin creates Offers such as:

**10% off first merchandise purchase**

**$5 off first Remix purchase**

**20% off first Creator month**

**15% off a Drop**

Customer incentive and Affiliate compensation are separate.

For example:

Customer receives:

**10% off**

Theo earns:

**12% eligible Affiliate commission**

The percentages do not need to match.

## Auto-Apply Links

A tracking link can preserve Affiliate attribution and automatically apply the relevant Offer where supported.

The customer might see:

> **Creator discount applied — THEO**

If someone manually enters **THEO**, the code establishes Theo's attribution.

## Creator Subscription Codes

The same system applies to Creator subscriptions.

Theo can say:

> **“Want to make your own? Use code THEO when you join CultRemix.”**

A viewer subscribes.

CultRemix records:

**Creator referred by Theo**

Theo receives the applicable recurring Creator referral commission.

## Attribution Conflicts

Attribution rules must be deterministic.

An initial rule:

> **An explicitly entered Affiliate code overrides an earlier first-party tracking-link attribution.**

External channels such as TikTok may impose their own attribution requirements, which CultRemix preserves where applicable.

---

# Affiliate Programs & Versioned Economics

Super Admin manages Affiliate Programs rather than configuring every Affiliate individually.

Programs can define:

- customer discount
- product commission
- Creator subscription commission
- subscription commission duration
- attribution window
- promo-code eligibility
- auto-apply behavior
- eligible products
- eligible categories
- eligible subscription plans
- start/end dates

Program terms are versioned.

Changes affect future applicable economic events without silently rewriting historical economics.

---

# Unified Earnings Ledger

CultRemix should not build separate payout systems for each type of earning.

All economic activity flows through a generalized **Economic Event / Earnings Ledger**.

Possible earning sources include:

- Brand royalty
- Source Creator royalty
- Creator royalty
- Variation Creator royalty
- Affiliate commission
- recurring Creator subscription commission
- gifts
- tips
- future sponsored rewards

Each earning records:

- source
- recipient
- economic role
- underlying order/subscription/event
- gross amount
- applicable costs
- distributable amount
- currency
- attribution source
- economic configuration version
- created date
- maturity date
- settlement status
- settlement batch
- Monad transaction

Earnings move through three primary states.

## Pending

The earning exists but remains inside its applicable

:::writing{variant="document" id="58314" title="CultRemix Product Thesis & V1 Build Plan"}
# CultRemix

## Product Thesis

**Culture itself is a remix.**

Every artist, designer, musician, filmmaker, writer, brand, and movement builds on what came before it. We borrow ideas, reinterpret aesthetics, combine influences, change context, and make things our own. We stand on the shoulders of the people who created before us, and culture moves forward because each generation remixes what it inherits.

The saying often attributed to Pablo Picasso captures the spirit: **“Good artists copy, great artists steal.”** The point isn't literal copying. Great creative work absorbs influences so completely that they become the raw material for something new.

AI changes the scale at which this can happen.

Take a great typography shirt. Historically, a designer creates one phrase in one visual treatment and sells that finished design.

**But what if that finished design isn't the end of the creative process? What if it's just the beginning?**

That same composition, typography, texture, illustration, and visual language could work with thousands of different phrases. One person might love the design but want completely different words. **Another might change the phrase and accidentally create the version that goes viral.**

**One great creative idea can become the starting point for thousands of others.**

Two technologies have now matured at exactly the right time to make that possible at scale.

**Generative AI dramatically expands what we can create and how quickly we can create it. At the same time, modern print-on-demand has become a high-quality, globally scalable manufacturing and fulfillment layer, allowing each new creation to become a real physical product without producing or holding inventory in advance.**

Now, we no longer have to manufacture one static interpretation of an idea and hope everyone wants the same thing.

**We can embrace infinite remixing.**

A Creator can make a great design and define which parts other people can make their own. Someone can remix the words and create a new Variation. A sufficiently transformative interpretation can fork into an entirely new creative root with its own remixable system. **Every finished Variation immediately becomes a real product that can be purchased, shared, promoted, and sold on CultRemix, with automatic syndication to major commerce channels.**

And CultRemix remembers where all of it came from.

> **CultRemix is a creative commerce platform where every work remembers where it came from and everyone who contributes to its value can participate in its economics.**

Creators publish remixable works. Other people create Variations or fork them into new creative roots. Brands can open portions of their identity to remixing. Affiliates distribute the resulting products. **CultRemix is built on the Monad blockchain, using permanent onchain provenance and programmable royalties to preserve creative lineage and ensure value flows back to the people who helped create it.**

## The Core Principle

> **Don’t just make something people like. Make something people want to make their own.**

That changes the goal of creation.

A successful CultRemix isn't merely something that gets likes. It's something that inspires participation.

**“I love that.” becomes “I want my version.”**

And every act of participation can create something new.

---

# Core Creative Model

CultRemix uses a small set of precise concepts.

## CultRemix

A **CultRemix** is a canonical remixable creative system or creative root.

It contains the creative work itself, its Remix schema, Creator, provenance, economics, products, and lineage.

## Variation

A **Variation** is a new expression created within an existing CultRemix's permitted Remix schema.

If a typography CultRemix allows the phrase and color to change, someone changing those variables creates a Variation.

The underlying creative system remains the same.

## Fork

A **Fork** intentionally uses an existing CultRemix as creative source material but establishes a sufficiently new creative system of its own.

A Fork receives a new CultRemix root and its own Remix schema.

It remains permanently connected to its direct parent through provenance.

## Creator

The person or entity that creates a CultRemix root.

## Variation Creator

The person who creates a particular Variation within an existing CultRemix.

## Source Creator

When one CultRemix is forked into another, the Creator of the **direct parent CultRemix** becomes the Source Creator of the new Fork.

The Source Creator participates economically in that Fork and its Variations.

## Brand / Rights Holder

An optional entity whose authorized Brand Assets or creative world are being used.

## Affiliate / Promoter

The person attributed with causing a qualifying transaction.

## Remix

**Remix** is primarily the action.

The user presses:

> **↻ REMIX THIS**

and ordinarily creates a **Variation**.

If they substantially transform the creative system itself, they can instead create a **Fork**, which becomes a new CultRemix root.

---

# The Creative Graph

Every published work receives a permanent CultRemix identity.

For example:

**CULTREMIX № 701-001**  
**AKA “Death Before Decaf”**

The number is canonical. The AKA is optional and human.

`701` identifies the creative family.

`001` identifies the Creator's canonical first expression of it.

Someone presses:

> **REMIX THIS ↻**

and changes the phrase.

The result might be:

**CULTREMIX № 701-002**  
**AKA “Sleep Is for Quitters”**

It remains inside the **701** creative family.

If someone substantially transforms CR 701 into a new remixable creative system, the result might become:

**CULTREMIX № 936-001**

with metadata:

> **Forked from CR № 701-042**

CR 936 now has its own Variations:

**936-002**  
**936-003**  
**936-004**

The creative graph might therefore look like:

**701-001**  
↓ Variation  
**701-002**  
↓ Variation  
**701-042**  
↓ Fork  
**936-001**  
↓ Variation  
**936-002**

And someone might eventually Fork 936:

**1204-001**  
*Forked from CR 936*

The complete ancestry remains visible forever:

**1204 → 936 → 701**

> **CultRemix remembers where creativity came from.**

---

# Infinite Attribution, Bounded Economics

This is a fundamental CultRemix principle:

> **Creative lineage can be infinitely deep. Economic inheritance is deliberately bounded.**

Suppose Sarah creates:

**CR 701**

Jon Forks it into:

**CR 936**

Sarah becomes the **Source Creator** of CR 936.

Sarah receives the applicable Source Creator Cut when:

- CR 936-001 sells
- CR 936-002 sells
- CR 936-300 sells
- any other qualifying Variation within the CR 936 family sells

Jon remains the Creator of CR 936.

If Mike later Forks CR 936 into:

**CR 1204**

Jon becomes the Source Creator of CR 1204.

Sarah remains permanently credited in the creative ancestry:

**1204 → 936 → 701**

but Sarah's Source Creator economics do **not** automatically cascade into CR 1204.

This creates a simple rule:

> **Source Creator economics travel exactly one Fork generation deep. Provenance travels forever.**

That prevents infinitely recursive royalty trees while ensuring someone cannot escape a Creator's economics simply by making enough changes to call something a Fork.

---

# Original, Variation, or Fork?

CultRemix should not pretend that AI can perfectly adjudicate originality.

Instead, publishing combines Creator declarations, structural rules, automated similarity analysis, and review when necessary.

## Variation

A work uses the existing CultRemix schema.

For example, if the existing schema permits:

**Phrase**  
**Color**

and someone changes those values, that is clearly a Variation.

## Fork

Someone intentionally starts from an existing CultRemix but changes the creative system itself.

They may change:

- composition
- visual hierarchy
- typography system
- illustrations
- graphic language
- available Remix variables
- relationships among those variables

The resulting work establishes its own Remix schema.

It becomes a new CultRemix root while retaining its parent relationship.

## Independent CultRemix

A Creator can also publish something as an independent creative root.

CultRemix can compare it against existing works using:

- visual similarity
- structural similarity
- typography similarity
- composition
- conceptual similarity
- Remix-schema similarity
- known source material

If a meaningful relationship appears, CultRemix can say:

> **Possible creative lineage detected: CR № 701**

The Creator can:

**Create as a Variation**

**Create as a Fork**

or

**Assert Independent Creation**

High-confidence or disputed cases can be reviewed.

---

# Lineage Corrections

Attribution will sometimes be discovered after publication.

Suppose:

**CR № 936-001**

is initially published as independent.

Later CultRemix determines that it substantially derives from:

**CR № 701-042**

CultRemix can establish:

**701-042 → 936-001**

without erasing history.

Monad retains the original provenance event and records a subsequent lineage amendment.

The record therefore accurately reflects:

> CR 936 was originally registered as independent.

and later:

> CR 936 was determined to be a Fork of CR 701.

Future economic events can use the corrected Source Creator relationship.

Historical settlements are not silently rewritten.

Economic configurations are versioned so the platform can determine which rules applied to each transaction at the time it occurred.

**History is preserved. Attribution improves.**

---

# Programmable Creative Economics

CultRemix recognizes that different people can contribute different kinds of value to the same cultural object.

For product commerce, there are five potential economic roles.

## 1. Brand / Rights Holder

Optional.

A Brand receives economics when authorized Brand Assets or Brand Worlds participate in the work.

## 2. Source Creator

Optional.

Exists when the current CultRemix is a Fork.

The Source Creator is the Creator of the direct parent CultRemix.

The Source Creator participates economically in the Fork and qualifying Variations belonging to that Fork's creative family.

Source Creator economics extend exactly one Fork generation.

## 3. Creator

The Creator of the current CultRemix root.

## 4. Variation Creator

Optional.

The person who created the specific Variation being purchased.

## 5. Affiliate / Promoter

Optional.

The person attributed with causing the transaction.

So a transaction can support:

> **Brand → Source Creator → Creator → Variation Creator → Affiliate**

Each role represents a different contribution.

One person or entity can occupy multiple roles.

CultRemix calculates the applicable distributable pool after transaction-specific commerce costs and allocates the configured shares.

> **CultRemix makes creative attribution programmable.**

---

# Example: Brand-Created World

Suppose a Brand creates an official CultRemix:

**CR 701-001**

Brand: **Brand X**

Creator: **Brand X**

Jon creates:

**CR 701-382**

Brand: **Brand X**

Creator: **Brand X**

Variation Creator: **Jon**

Theo promotes it and causes a sale.

Affiliate: **Theo**

Brand X occupies both Brand and Creator roles.

Jon receives Variation Creator economics.

Theo receives Affiliate economics.

---

# Example: Fork Economics

Jon creates a permitted Fork:

**CR 936-001**

derived from:

**CR 701**

The economics can now include:

Brand: **Brand X**

Source Creator: **Brand X / CR 701**

Creator: **Jon / CR 936**

Sarah then creates:

**CR 936-204**

Now:

Brand: **Brand X**

Source Creator: **Brand X / CR 701**

Creator: **Jon / CR 936**

Variation Creator: **Sarah**

Affiliate: **Theo**

All five economic roles are now populated for one transaction.

If CR 936 is later Forked into CR 1204, Jon becomes Source Creator for 1204.

The earlier Source Creator relationship does not economically cascade another generation.

> **Lineage can be infinite. Economics remain bounded.**

---

# Brand Worlds

Brands can eventually open CultRemix Worlds.

Rather than simply protecting every part of an identity, a Brand can say:

> **“Here’s the part of our identity we’re inviting culture to play with.”**

A Brand World can contain approved:

- logos
- word marks
- phrases
- characters
- visual systems
- likenesses
- photography
- graphic elements
- other Brand Assets

Each asset can have Remix permissions.

Possible modes include:

**Open**

Anyone can use the asset within defined rules.

**Approval Required**

Creators can create with it, but commercial publication requires Brand approval.

**Invited Creators**

Only selected Creators can publish commercial work.

**Closed**

The asset is protected from third-party commercial use within CultRemix.

Brands can also determine whether:

- Variations are allowed
- Forks are allowed
- Brand approval is required
- products can be sold
- certain categories are prohibited
- specific royalty configurations apply

A Brand can elevate exceptional community work to:

> **OFFICIAL ✓**

This turns Brand identity into programmable creative material.

---

# Creative Rights & Brand Assets

CultRemix distinguishes between three related systems.

## Creative Lineage

Who created this creative system and what did it descend from?

## Brand Assets

Whose authorized logo, phrase, visual identity, likeness, character, or other Brand material is incorporated?

## External Rights

Does outside intellectual property potentially exist that CultRemix needs to recognize?

CultRemix can use automated analysis and external search systems to detect:

- trademarked phrases
- logos
- recognizable Brand marks
- substantially similar existing CultRemixes
- potentially protected external material

High-confidence issues can be blocked or routed for review.

Ambiguous cases can use a rights-holder reporting and review process.

CultRemix should never imply that paying to register a phrase internally grants legal ownership of that phrase.

Platform Brand Asset rights and actual trademark rights remain distinct.

---

# Affiliate & Creator Referral Economics

Affiliate economics extend beyond product sales.

When Creators livestream, post creation videos, share their work, promote their profiles, or simply demonstrate what they're making, they are also marketing CultRemix.

Someone may watch and think:

> **“I want to do this too.”**

If that person becomes a paying CultRemix Creator, the person who caused that conversion created measurable ecosystem value.

CultRemix therefore supports two major Affiliate earning categories.

## Commerce Referrals

An Affiliate causes a customer to purchase a product.

The Affiliate receives the applicable product commission.

## Creator Referrals

An Affiliate causes someone to become a paying Creator.

The Affiliate receives a **recurring share of eligible Creator subscription revenue** under the applicable referral program.

The exact percentage and duration remain configurable.

For example:

> **20% of referred Creator subscription revenue for 12 months.**

Referral attribution is direct rather than multilevel.

If Sarah refers Jane and Jane later refers Mike:

**Sarah earns from Jane.**

**Jane earns from Mike.**

Sarah does not receive an override from Mike.

The Creator proposition becomes:

> **Create. Get remixed. Get paid.**

Creators can earn when:

- their CultRemix sells
- Variations within their creative family sell
- their own Variations sell
- their direct Fork descendants generate qualifying sales
- they drive product sales
- they directly inspire paying Creators to join CultRemix

---

# Affiliate Links, Codes & Offers

Every eligible Creator or Affiliate can have simple attribution tools for social posts, livestreams, Stories, profiles, videos, and offline promotion.

Two primary mechanisms represent the same Affiliate identity.

## Tracking Link

Example:

`cultremix.com/r/701?ref=theo`

## Affiliate Code

Example:

**THEO**

The code simultaneously provides:

### Customer Incentive

> **Use THEO for 10% off.**

### Affiliate Attribution

The qualifying transaction is attributed to Theo.

Codes are especially useful when a link isn't practical.

Someone may:

- watch a livestream on television
- hear a Creator say the code
- change devices
- discover a repost
- remember a Creator's name
- visit CultRemix later directly

The code preserves attribution.

## Short Codes

Creators can provide a preferred base:

**Theo**

CultRemix generates the shortest appropriate available code:

**THEO**

If unavailable:

**THEO10**

or another concise approved alternative.

CultRemix controls final code issuance and reserves misleading, administrative, platform, and unauthorized Brand terms.

## Super Admin Controls Offers

Affiliates do not determine discounts.

Super Admin creates Offers such as:

**10% off first merchandise purchase**

**$5 off first Remix purchase**

**20% off first Creator month**

**15% off a Drop**

Customer incentive and Affiliate compensation are separate.

For example:

Customer receives:

**10% off**

Theo earns:

**12% eligible Affiliate commission**

The percentages do not need to match.

## Auto-Apply Links

A tracking link can preserve Affiliate attribution and automatically apply the relevant Offer where supported.

The customer might see:

> **Creator discount applied — THEO**

If someone manually enters **THEO**, the code establishes Theo's attribution.

## Creator Subscription Codes

The same system applies to Creator subscriptions.

Theo can say:

> **“Want to make your own? Use code THEO when you join CultRemix.”**

A viewer subscribes.

CultRemix records:

**Creator referred by Theo**

Theo receives the applicable recurring Creator referral commission.

## Attribution Conflicts

Attribution rules must be deterministic.

An initial rule:

> **An explicitly entered Affiliate code overrides an earlier first-party tracking-link attribution.**

External channels such as TikTok may impose their own attribution requirements, which CultRemix preserves where applicable.

---

# Affiliate Programs & Versioned Economics

Super Admin manages Affiliate Programs rather than configuring every Affiliate individually.

Programs can define:

- customer discount
- product commission
- Creator subscription commission
- subscription commission duration
- attribution window
- promo-code eligibility
- auto-apply behavior
- eligible products
- eligible categories
- eligible subscription plans
- start and end dates

Program terms are versioned.

Changes affect future applicable economic events without silently rewriting historical economics.

---

# Unified Earnings Ledger

CultRemix should not build separate payout systems for each type of earning.

All economic activity flows through a generalized **Economic Event / Earnings Ledger**.

Possible earning sources include:

- Brand royalty
- Source Creator royalty
- Creator royalty
- Variation Creator royalty
- Affiliate commission
- recurring Creator subscription commission
- gifts
- tips
- future sponsored rewards

Each earning records:

- source
- recipient
- economic role
- underlying order, subscription, or event
- gross amount
- applicable costs
- distributable amount
- currency
- attribution source
- economic configuration version
- created date
- maturity date
- settlement status
- settlement batch
- Monad transaction

Earnings move through three primary states.

## Pending

The earning exists but remains inside its applicable refund, return, dispute,

It remains within creative family **701**.

Additional Variations become:

**701-003**  
**701-004**  
**701-005**

and so on.

The number becomes a durable identity that can travel with the work across CultRemix, physical merchandise, social media, Drops, and commerce channels.

---

# 3. The Creative Graph

Suppose someone substantially transforms:

**CR 701-042**

into a new creative system.

That becomes:

## CR 936-001

with:

> **Forked from CR 701-042**

CR 936 now develops its own Variations:

**936-002**  
**936-003**  
**936-004**

Someone can later Fork CR 936 into:

**CR 1204-001**

The complete ancestry remains visible:

**1204 → 936 → 701**

CultRemix therefore maintains a living graph showing how creative ideas evolve.

> **CultRemix remembers where creativity came from.**

---

# 4. Infinite Attribution, Bounded Economics

A fundamental CultRemix rule:

> **Creative lineage can be infinitely deep. Economic inheritance is deliberately bounded.**

Suppose Sarah creates:

**CR 701**

Jon Forks it into:

**CR 936**

Sarah becomes the **Source Creator** of CR 936.

Sarah receives the applicable Source Creator Cut when:

- 936-001 sells
- 936-002 sells
- 936-300 sells
- any other qualifying Variation within the 936 family sells

Jon remains the Creator of CR 936.

Now Mike Forks CR 936 into:

**CR 1204**

Jon becomes Source Creator of CR 1204.

Sarah remains permanently visible in the ancestry:

**1204 → 936 → 701**

but Sarah's Source Creator economics do not automatically cascade into 1204.

The rule is:

> **Source Creator economics travel exactly one Fork generation deep. Provenance travels forever.**

This prevents infinitely recursive royalty trees while ensuring someone cannot escape a Creator's economics simply by changing enough elements to call something a Fork.

---

# 5. Original, Variation, or Fork?

CultRemix should not pretend AI can perfectly adjudicate originality.

Instead, classification combines:

- structural rules
- Creator declarations
- automated similarity analysis
- provenance
- human review where necessary

## Variation

The new work operates within the existing Remix schema.

If the schema permits phrase and color changes and someone changes those variables, it is a Variation.

## Fork

The person intentionally starts from an existing CultRemix but substantially changes the creative system itself.

The result establishes its own Remix schema and becomes a new CultRemix root.

## Independent CultRemix

A Creator can publish a new independent root.

Before publication, CultRemix can compare it with existing works using:

- visual similarity
- structural similarity
- typography similarity
- composition
- conceptual similarity
- Remix-schema similarity
- known source material

If a meaningful relationship appears:

> **Possible creative lineage detected: CR № 701**

The Creator can choose:

**Create as Variation**

**Create as Fork**

**Assert Independent Creation**

High-confidence or disputed cases can be reviewed.

---

# 6. Lineage Corrections

Creative relationships will sometimes be discovered after publication.

Suppose:

**CR 936-001**

is initially registered as independent.

Later CultRemix determines that it substantially derives from:

**CR 701-042**

CultRemix establishes:

**701-042 → 936-001**

without erasing history.

Monad preserves the original provenance event and records a subsequent lineage amendment.

The historical record therefore truthfully shows:

> CR 936 was originally registered as independent.

and later:

> CR 936 was determined to be a Fork of CR 701.

Future economic events can use the corrected Source Creator relationship.

Historical settlements are not silently rewritten.

Economic configurations are versioned so CultRemix always knows which rules governed each transaction.

**History is preserved. Attribution improves.**

---

# 7. Programmable Creative Economics

CultRemix recognizes that different people can contribute different kinds of value to the same cultural object.

A product transaction can support five economic roles.

## 1. Brand / Rights Holder

Optional.

Receives economics when authorized Brand Assets or Worlds participate in the work.

## 2. Source Creator

Optional.

Exists when the current CultRemix is a Fork.

The Source Creator is the Creator of its direct parent CultRemix and participates economically in the Fork and its Variations.

## 3. Creator

The Creator of the current CultRemix root.

## 4. Variation Creator

Optional.

The person who created the specific Variation being purchased.

## 5. Affiliate / Promoter

Optional.

The person attributed with causing the transaction.

The potential economic chain is therefore:

> **Brand → Source Creator → Creator → Variation Creator → Affiliate**

Each role represents a different contribution.

One person or entity can occupy multiple roles.

> **CultRemix makes creative attribution programmable.**

---

# 8. Example Economics

Suppose Brand X creates:

**CR 701-001**

Brand: **Brand X**

Creator: **Brand X**

Jon creates:

**CR 701-382**

Brand: **Brand X**

Creator: **Brand X**

Variation Creator: **Jon**

Theo promotes it and causes a sale.

Affiliate: **Theo**

Brand X occupies both Brand and Creator roles.

Jon participates for creating the Variation.

Theo participates for distribution.

Now Jon creates a permitted Fork:

**CR 936-001**

derived from CR 701.

The potential economics become:

Brand: **Brand X**

Source Creator: **Brand X / CR 701**

Creator: **Jon / CR 936**

Sarah creates:

**CR 936-204**

and Theo sells it.

Now all five roles are populated:

> **Brand X → Brand**  
> **Brand X / CR 701 → Source Creator**  
> **Jon / CR 936 → Creator**  
> **Sarah / CR 936-204 → Variation Creator**  
> **Theo → Affiliate**

If CR 936 is later Forked into CR 1204, Jon becomes Source Creator for CR 1204.

Brand rights can continue separately if the Brand Assets remain involved, while Source Creator economics remain one Fork generation deep.

---

# 9. Creator Studio

Commercial publishing requires a Creator subscription.

Creators aren't paying merely for the right to list products.

They're subscribing to an:

> **AI-powered creative business studio.**

Membership provides monthly generation credits.

The workflow:

> **IDEA → CREATE → REMIXIFY → PUBLISH → MERCHANDISE → MARKET → EARN**

## Create

A Creator can begin with:

- a prompt
- an image
- one influence
- multiple influences
- an existing CultRemix
- an authorized Brand World

AI helps develop the idea into finished creative work.

## Remixify

CultRemix analyzes the finished design and proposes its structure.

For example:

### Editable

- headline
- subtitle
- person
- pet
- color
- location

### Locked

- composition
- typography treatment
- graphic system
- illustration style

The Creator approves or edits the schema.

This is the moment a finished image becomes a:

> **programmable creative object.**

---

# 10. Publish

The Creator presses:

## PUBLISH REMIX

CultRemix automatically:

- assigns the CR number
- registers provenance
- creates the optional AKA
- writes a description
- generates tags
- assigns categories
- creates the public Remix page
- establishes lineage
- establishes economic relationships
- generates production artwork
- creates product mockups
- creates editorial imagery
- generates social assets
- creates commerce listings
- makes the finished Variation immediately purchasable
- makes permitted elements immediately remixable

Publishing means:

> **Turn this creative idea into an economic object.**

---

# 11. Product Studio

CultRemix should not look like generic print-on-demand merchandise.

It should develop a recognizable:

> **CultRemix editorial photography language.**

AI determines how each creative work should be presented while staying inside the overall CultRemix visual system.

A rock-inspired design may be cast and photographed differently from romantic typography, Christian streetwear, or retro graphics, while everything still feels recognizably CultRemix.

Publishing can automatically create:

- hero editorial imagery
- product mockups
- garment details
- alternate models
- vertical imagery
- marketplace thumbnails
- print-ready files

The Creator shouldn't need to art-direct a new photoshoot every time they publish.

---

# 12. Marketing Studio

Creation doesn't stop when the product exists.

CultRemix AI helps Creators market what they make.

For each CultRemix it can generate:

- TikTok concepts
- Reels concepts
- hooks
- captions
- vertical videos
- animated reveals
- Stories
- Pinterest creative
- thumbnails
- Affiliate assets
- Creator-story scripts
- descriptions
- tags
- SEO metadata

A Creator might say:

> **“I found this old 1970s typography treatment and mixed it with this weird vintage motel-sign aesthetic. I ended up making this.”**

Then reveal:

> **“It's CR № 701-001. You can buy mine or Remix it with your own phrase. Either way, it supports me.”**

That content simultaneously markets:

**the Creator**

**the CultRemix**

**the ability to Remix**

**CultRemix itself**

**the opportunity to become a Creator**

A Creator's normal creative activity therefore naturally becomes distribution for the platform.

---

# 13. Creator Subscriptions & Credits

Creator subscriptions fund the AI infrastructure required to create.

Possible tiers:

## Creator

Regular monthly generation allowance.

## Creator Pro

Higher allowance plus advanced creation and marketing capabilities.

## Studio

Large allowances, teams, analytics, Brand tooling, and advanced automation.

Credits primarily meter computationally expensive operations:

**Image generation → credits**

**Premium generation → more credits**

**Mockup generation → credits**

**Social campaign → credits**

**Video → more credits**

**Music/audio → more credits**

CultRemix should generally avoid charging credits for ordinary commerce behavior such as publishing, selling, viewing analytics, or receiving royalties.

> **CultRemix makes money when Creators create and when Creators succeed, not by nickel-and-diming the act of selling.**

---

# 14. Affiliate & Creator Referral Economics

Affiliate economics extend beyond product sales.

When Creators livestream, post creation videos, share their work, or promote their CultRemix profiles, they're also demonstrating the platform itself.

Someone may watch and think:

> **“I want to do this too.”**

If that person becomes a paying Creator, the person who inspired the conversion created measurable value.

CultRemix therefore supports two major Affiliate earning categories.

## Product Referrals

An Affiliate causes a qualifying product sale and receives the applicable commission.

## Creator Referrals

An Affiliate causes someone to become a paying Creator and receives a:

> **recurring share of eligible Creator subscription revenue.**

Percentage and duration remain configurable.

For example:

> **20% for 12 months**

Referral economics are direct rather than multilevel.

If Sarah refers Jane and Jane refers Mike:

**Sarah earns from Jane.**

**Jane earns from Mike.**

Sarah does not receive an override from Mike.

This keeps Creator acquisition economics understandable and avoids MLM-style referral structures.

---

# 15. Affiliate Links, Codes & Offers

Creators and Affiliates receive simple attribution tools that work naturally in videos, livestreams, Stories, profiles, and conversations.

## Tracking Link

Example:

`cultremix.com/?ref=theo`

## Affiliate Code

Example:

**THEO**

Both represent the same Affiliate identity.

The code simultaneously provides:

### Customer Incentive

> **Use THEO for 10% off.**

### Affiliate Attribution

The qualifying transaction is attributed to Theo.

This matters when someone:

- watches a livestream
- hears a code spoken aloud
- changes devices
- sees a repost
- visits CultRemix later
- remembers the Creator's name

## Short Codes

The Affiliate provides a preferred base:

**Theo**

CultRemix generates the shortest appropriate available code:

**THEO**

or, if unavailable:

**THEO10**

CultRemix controls final issuance and reserves inappropriate, misleading, administrative, platform, and unauthorized Brand terms.

---

# 16. Offers

Affiliates do not determine the discount economics.

Super Admin creates Offers such as:

**10% off first merchandise purchase**

**$5 off first qualifying purchase**

**20% off first Creator month**

**15% off a Drop**

Customer incentive and Affiliate compensation remain separate.

For example:

**Customer receives:** 10% off

**Theo earns:** 12% eligible commission

Those percentages do not need to match.

Tracking links can automatically preserve Affiliate attribution and apply the relevant Offer where technically supported.

If the customer manually enters **THEO**, the code establishes Theo's attribution.

For initial first-party attribution conflicts:

> **An explicitly entered Affiliate code overrides an earlier first-party tracking-link attribution.**

External channels can impose their own attribution rules, which CultRemix preserves when required.

---

# 17. Affiliate Programs

Super Admin manages Affiliate Programs rather than individually configuring every Affiliate.

Programs can define:

- customer benefit
- product commission
- Creator subscription commission
- subscription commission duration
- attribution

---

## 134 · ChatGPT · 2026-09-23 17:28:58 PDT

<!-- source-message-id: af5b8f37-30e3-47f0-ad17-1b84159cecc5 -->

Yes. **“on CultRemix.com, with automatic syndication…”** makes the relationship much clearer: CultRemix.com is the canonical home, and external commerce channels are distribution.

I also normalized the capitalization so ordinary terms like creator, variation, fork, and affiliate read naturally, while formal headings and named product concepts remain clear.

:::writing{variant="document" id="58314" title="CultRemix — Product Thesis, Architecture & V1 Build Plan"}
# CultRemix

## Product Thesis

**Culture itself is a remix.**

Every artist, designer, musician, filmmaker, writer, brand, and movement builds on what came before it. We borrow ideas, reinterpret aesthetics, combine influences, change context, and make things our own. We stand on the shoulders of the people who created before us, and culture moves forward because each generation remixes what it inherits.

The saying often attributed to Pablo Picasso captures the spirit: **“Good artists copy, great artists steal.”** The point isn't literal copying. Great creative work absorbs influences so completely that they become the raw material for something new.

AI changes the scale at which this can happen.

Take a great typography shirt. Historically, a designer creates one phrase in one visual treatment and sells that finished design.

**But what if that finished design isn't the end of the creative process? What if it's just the beginning?**

That same composition, typography, texture, illustration, and visual language could work with thousands of different phrases. One person might love the design but want completely different words. **Another might change the phrase and accidentally create the version that goes viral.**

**One great creative idea can become the starting point for thousands of others.**

Two technologies have now matured at exactly the right time to make that possible at scale.

**Generative AI dramatically expands what we can create and how quickly we can create it. At the same time, modern print-on-demand has become a high-quality, globally scalable manufacturing and fulfillment layer, allowing each new creation to become a real physical product without producing or holding inventory in advance.**

Now, we no longer have to manufacture one static interpretation of an idea and hope everyone wants the same thing.

**We can embrace infinite remixing.**

A creator can make a great design and define which parts other people can make their own. **In a typography design, for example, someone could remix the words and create a new variation.** In other works, the remixable elements might be people, images, colors, characters, graphics, or other parts of the creative system. A sufficiently transformative interpretation can fork into an entirely new creative root with its own remixable system.

**Every finished CultRemix, whether an original or a variation, immediately becomes a real product that can be purchased, shared, promoted, and sold on CultRemix.com, with automatic syndication to major commerce channels such as TikTok Shop, Shop, Amazon, Etsy, and other supported channels.**

And CultRemix remembers where all of it came from.

> **CultRemix is a creative commerce platform where every work remembers where it came from and everyone who contributes to its value can participate in its economics.**

Creators publish remixable works. Other people create variations or fork them into new creative roots. Brands can open portions of their identity to remixing. Affiliates distribute the resulting products.

**CultRemix is built on the Monad blockchain, using permanent onchain provenance and programmable royalties to preserve creative lineage and ensure value flows back to the people who helped create it.**

---

# The Core Principle

> **Don’t just make something people like. Make something people want to make their own.**

That changes the goal of creation.

A successful CultRemix isn't merely something that gets likes. It's something that inspires participation.

**“I love that.” becomes “I want my version.”**

Every act of participation can create something new.

---

# 1. The Creative Model

CultRemix uses a small number of precise concepts.

## CultRemix

A **CultRemix** is a canonical remixable creative system or creative root.

It contains:

- the creative work
- its creator
- its remix schema
- its variations
- its lineage
- its rights
- its economic configuration
- its products
- its provenance

## Variation

A **variation** is a new expression created within an existing CultRemix's permitted remix schema.

For example, a typography CultRemix might allow:

- phrase
- subtitle
- color

to change while preserving its composition, typography treatment, texture, and visual system.

Changing those permitted variables creates a variation.

The underlying creative system remains the same.

## Fork

A **fork** intentionally uses an existing CultRemix as creative source material but establishes a sufficiently new creative system of its own.

A fork might substantially change:

- composition
- visual hierarchy
- typography system
- illustration
- graphic language
- available remix variables
- relationships among those variables

A fork receives:

- a new CultRemix root
- its own remix schema
- its own creator
- permanent provenance connecting it to its direct parent

## Creator

The person or entity that creates a CultRemix root.

## Variation Creator

The person who creates a particular variation.

## Source Creator

When one CultRemix is forked into another, the creator of the **direct parent CultRemix** becomes the source creator of the new fork.

The source creator participates economically in that fork and its variations.

## Brand / Rights Holder

An optional entity whose authorized brand assets or creative world are being used.

## Affiliate / Promoter

The person attributed with causing a qualifying transaction.

## Remix

**Remix is primarily the action.**

The user presses:

> **↻ REMIX THIS**

and ordinarily creates a variation.

If they substantially transform the creative system itself, they can instead create a fork, which becomes a new CultRemix root.

---

`001` is the creator's canonical first expression.

### Locked

- composition
- typography treatment
- graphic system
- illustration style

The creator approves or edits the schema.

This is the moment a finished image becomes a:

> **programmable creative object.**

---

# 10. Publish

The creator presses:

## PUBLISH REMIX

CultRemix automatically:

- assigns the CR number
- registers provenance
- creates the optional AKA
- writes a description
- generates tags
- assigns categories
- creates the public Remix page
- establishes lineage
- establishes economic relationships
- generates production artwork
- creates product mockups
- creates editorial imagery
- generates social assets
- creates commerce listings
- makes the finished CultRemix immediately purchasable
- makes permitted elements immediately remixable
- syndicates eligible products to supported commerce channels

Publishing means:

> **Turn this creative idea into an economic object.**

---

# 11. Product Studio

CultRemix should not look like generic print-on-demand merchandise.

It should develop a recognizable:

> **CultRemix editorial photography language.**

AI determines how each creative work should be presented while staying inside the overall CultRemix visual system.

A rock-inspired design may be cast and photographed differently from romantic typography, Christian streetwear, or retro graphics, while everything still feels recognizably CultRemix.

Publishing can automatically create:

- hero editorial imagery
- product mockups
- garment details
- alternate models
- vertical imagery
- marketplace thumbnails
- print-ready files

The creator shouldn't need to art-direct a new photoshoot every time they publish.

---

# 12. Marketing Studio

Creation doesn't stop when the product exists.

CultRemix AI helps creators market what they make.

For each CultRemix it can generate:

- TikTok concepts
- Reels concepts
- hooks
- captions
- vertical videos
- animated reveals
- Stories
- Pinterest creative
- thumbnails
- affiliate assets
- creator-story scripts
- descriptions
- tags
- SEO metadata

A creator might say:

> **“I found this old 1970s typography treatment and mixed it with this weird vintage motel-sign aesthetic. I ended up making this.”**

Then reveal:

> **“It's CR № 701-001. You can buy mine or Remix it with your own phrase. Either way, it supports me.”**

That content simultaneously markets:

**the creator**

**the CultRemix**

**the ability to Remix**

**CultRemix itself**

**the opportunity to become a creator**

A creator's normal creative activity therefore naturally becomes distribution for the platform.

---

# 13. Creator Subscriptions & Credits

Creator subscriptions fund the AI infrastructure required to create.

Possible tiers:

## Creator

Regular monthly generation allowance.

## Creator Pro

Higher allowance plus advanced creation and marketing capabilities.

## Studio

Large allowances, teams, analytics, Brand tooling, and advanced automation.

Credits primarily meter computationally expensive operations:

**Image generation → credits**

**Premium generation → more credits**

**Mockup generation → credits**

**Social campaign → credits**

**Video → more credits**

**Music/audio → more credits**

CultRemix should generally avoid charging credits for ordinary commerce behavior such as publishing, selling, viewing analytics, or receiving royalties.

> **CultRemix makes money when creators create and when creators succeed, not by nickel-and-diming the act of selling.**

---

# 14. Affiliate & Creator Referral Economics

Affiliate economics extend beyond product sales.

When creators livestream, post creation videos, share their work, or promote their CultRemix profiles, they're also demonstrating the platform itself.

Someone may watch and think:

> **“I want to do this too.”**

If that person becomes a paying creator, the person who inspired the conversion created measurable value.

CultRemix therefore supports two major affiliate earning categories.

## Product Referrals

An affiliate causes a qualifying product sale and receives the applicable commission.

## Creator Referrals

An affiliate causes someone to become a paying creator and receives a:

> **recurring share of eligible creator subscription revenue.**

Percentage and duration remain configurable.

For example:

> **20% for 12 months**

Referral economics are direct rather than multilevel.

If Sarah refers Jane and Jane refers Mike:

**Sarah earns from Jane.**

**Jane earns from Mike.**

Sarah does not receive an override from Mike.

This keeps creator acquisition economics understandable and avoids MLM-style referral structures.

---

# 15. Affiliate Links, Codes & Offers

Creators and affiliates receive simple attribution tools that work naturally in videos, livestreams, Stories, profiles, and conversations.

## Tracking Link

Example:

`cultremix.com/?ref=theo`

## Affiliate Code

Example:

**THEO**

Both represent the same affiliate identity.

The code simultaneously provides:

### Customer Incentive

> **Use THEO for 10% off.**

### Affiliate Attribution

The qualifying transaction is attributed to Theo.

This matters when someone:

- watches a livestream
- hears a code spoken aloud
- changes devices
- sees a repost
- visits CultRemix later
- remembers the creator's name

## Short Codes

The affiliate provides a preferred base:

**Theo**

CultRemix generates the shortest appropriate available code:

**THEO**

or, if unavailable:

**THEO10**

CultRemix controls final issuance and reserves inappropriate, misleading, administrative, platform, and unauthorized brand terms.

---

# 16. Offers

Affiliates do not determine discount economics.

Super Admin creates offers such as:

**10% off first merchandise purchase**

**$5 off first qualifying purchase**

**20% off first creator month**

**15% off a Drop**

Customer incentive and affiliate compensation remain separate.

For example:

**Customer receives:** 10% off

**Theo earns:** 12% eligible commission

Those percentages do not need to match.

Tracking links can automatically preserve affiliate attribution and apply the relevant offer where technically supported.

If the customer manually enters **THEO**, the code establishes Theo's attribution.

For

---
